Definition 3.3
.

The binary relation
=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}

on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
defined by
P=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}Q\iff\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}(P)=\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}(Q)

is called
free full
\bm{{\sf U}}
-valuation congruence
.

Lemma 3.4
.

The relation
=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}
is a congruence.

Proof.

Cf. Lemma
2.4
.
∎

Definition 3.5
.

A
Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic with undefinedness
(
\textup{$\textrm{FEL}$}^{\sf U}
)
is a logic that
satisfies the consequences of
\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}
-equality.

Free Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic with undefinedness
(
\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}
)
is the fully evaluated
left-sequential logic with undefinedness that satisfies no more consequences than those of

\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}
-equality, i.e., for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{FEL}$}^{\sf U}\models P=Q\Longleftarrow P=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}Q\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}\models P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}Q.

In order to axiomatise
\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}
, we extend
EqFFEL
(Table
1
) as follows:

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}=\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\cup\{\neg{\sf U}={\sf U},~{\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}\}.

Lemma 3.6

(Soundness)
.

For all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P=Q~\Longrightarrow~\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}\models P=Q
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.4
, the relation
=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}
is a congruence on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,
so it suffices to show that all closed instances of the
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
-axioms satisfy
=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}
,
which follows easily (cf. the proof of Lemma
2.7
).
The validity of the two new axioms of
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
is also easily verified.
∎

Defining
{\sf U}^{dl}={\sf U}
, it also immediately follows that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
satisfies the duality principle.
Furthermore, defining
x^{dl}=x

for each variable
x
, the duality principle extends to equations,
that is, for all terms
s,t
over
\Sigma_{\textup{$\textrm{FEL}$}}^{\sf U}(A)
,

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash s=t\iff\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash s^{dl}=t^{dl}.

We start with some useful consequences of
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

The following equations are consequences of
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
:

1.

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}
,

2.

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})
,

3.

\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})
.

Proof.

Note that by duality,
{\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}
. We first derive two auxiliary results:

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}

\displaystyle=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{FFEL10}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U},

(Aux1)

\displaystyle\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}

\displaystyle=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}.

(Aux2)

Consequence 1:
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{A.1}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{A.1}}}{{=}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}
.

Consequence 2:

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{FFEL10}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{A.1}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{A.1}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})
.

Consequence 3: by consequences
1
and
2
,
(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})
,
hence

\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})=(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{A.2}}}{{=}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})
.
∎

We introduce “
{\sf U}
-normal forms” and prove a few lemmas from which our next
completeness result follows easily.

Definition 3.8
.

For
\sigma\in A^{*}
and
a\in A
,

\bm{{\sf U}_{\sigma}}
is defined by

{\sf U}_{\epsilon}={\sf U}
and

{\sf U}_{a\rho}=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho}.

Lemma 3.9
.

For all
\sigma\in A^{*}
,

{\sf U}_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}_{\sigma}
and

{\sf U}_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}_{\sigma}
are consequences of
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Proof.

The first consequence follows easily by induction on the length of
\sigma
and
associativity (
FFEL4
),
as well as
({\sf U}_{\sigma})^{dl}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
. By the latter,

{\sf U}_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}_{\sigma}
.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

For all
\sigma\in A^{*}
,
the following equations are consequences of
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}:

1.

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}
,

2.

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})
,

3.

\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})
.

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on the length of
\sigma
.
The base case (
\sigma=\epsilon
) is Lemma
3.7
.1-3.
For
\sigma=a\rho~(a\in A)
, derive

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{a\rho})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH2}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho}))=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{a\rho}
,

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{a\rho})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH2}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{a\rho})
,

\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{a\rho})=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH3}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{a\rho})
.
∎

To prove the forthcoming completeness result, it suffices to restrict
to Negation Normal Forms (NNFs), which are defined as follows (
a\in A
):

\displaystyle P::={\sf T}\mid{\sf F}\mid{\sf U}\mid a\mid\neg a\mid P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P\mid P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P.

By structural induction it immediately follows that for each
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
, there is a unique NNF

Q
such that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P=Q
.

Lemma 3.11
.

For each
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
and
\sigma\in A^{*}
, there exists
\rho\in A^{*}
s.t.

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\rho}
.

Proof.

By structural induction, restricting to NNFs. The base cases
{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\sigma}

and
\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{a\sigma}

follow by Lemma
3.10
.3, and the other base cases are trivial.
For the induction there are two cases:

Case
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
. Derive
(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\text{IH}}}}{{=}}Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH}}}{{=}}{\sf U}_{\rho}

for some
\rho^{\prime},\rho\in A^{*}
.

Case
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
. By Lemma
3.10
.1 and induction,

(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma})=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho^{\prime}}

for some
\rho^{\prime}\in A^{*}
, and
by Lemma
3.10
.2 and induction,
Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho^{\prime}}=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho^{\prime}}={\sf U}_{\rho}

for some
\rho\in A^{*}
.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

For each
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
that contains
{\sf U}
, there exists

\sigma\in A^{*}
s.t.
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P={\sf U}_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

By structural induction, restricting to NNFs. The only base case is
P={\sf U}={\sf U}_{\epsilon}
.
For the induction there are two cases:

Case
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
. Apply a case distinction:
if
{\sf U}
occurs in
Q
, then by induction,
Q={\sf U}_{\sigma}

and by Lemma
3.9
,
P={\sf U}_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R={\sf U}_{\sigma}
;
if
{\sf U}
does not occur in
Q
, then
{\sf U}
occurs in
R
and by induction,
R={\sf U}_{\sigma}
,
so
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}
. By Lemma
3.11
,
Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\rho}

for some
\rho\in A^{*}
.

Case
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
. Apply a case distinction:
if
{\sf U}
occurs in
Q
, then by induction,
Q={\sf U}_{\sigma}
, and
by Lemma
3.9
,
P={\sf U}_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R={\sf U}_{\sigma}
;
if
{\sf U}
does not occur in
Q
, then
{\sf U}
occurs in
R
and by induction,
R={\sf U}_{\sigma}
,
so,
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}
.
By Lemma
3.10
.2 and Lemma
3.11
,

Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\rho}

for some
\rho\in A^{*}
.
∎

Theorem 3.13

(Completeness)
.

The logic
\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}
is axiomatised by
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.6
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
is sound.
For completeness, assume
P_{1}=_{\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}}P_{2}
. Then, either
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
do not contain
{\sf U}
and by Theorem
2.19

we are done, or both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
contain
{\sf U}
.
By Lemma
3.12
, there are
{\sf U}_{\sigma_{i}}\in A^{*}
such that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P_{i}={\sf U}_{\sigma_{i}}
.
By assumption and soundness,
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}
.
Hence
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P_{1}={\sf U}_{\sigma_{1}}=P_{2}
.
∎

4
Memorising FEL (
MFEL
)

In this section we define and axiomatise “Memorising FEL” (
MFEL
). As noted in the Introduction,
the logic
MFEL
is based on the evaluation strategy in which the evaluation of each atom
is memorised:
no complete path in a memorising evaluation tree contains multiple occurrences of the same atom.
Memorising evaluation trees appear to be fundamental to stronger logics
such as
C
\ell
FEL
2
(Section
6
) and several short-circuit logics defined in
[
6
,
3
]
.
We present equational axioms for the equality of memorising evaluation trees and prove
a completeness result.

Definition 4.1
.

The evaluation trees
{\sf T},{\sf F}\in\mathcal{T}_{A}
are
memorising evaluation trees
.

The evaluation tree
(X\unlhd a\unrhd Y)\in\mathcal{T}_{A}
is a
memorising evaluation tree
over
A

if both
X
and
Y
are memorising evaluation trees that do not contain the label
a
.

We interpret propositional expressions in
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
as memorising evaluation trees
by a function
\mathit{mfe}
.

Definition 4.2
.

The unary
memorising full evaluation
function
\mathit{mfe}:{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}\to\mathcal{T}_{A}

is defined by

\mathit{mfe}(P)=\mathit{m}(\mathit{fe}(P)),

where the auxiliary function
\mathit{m}:\mathcal{T}_{A}\to\mathcal{T}_{A}
is defined as
follows, for
a\in A
:

\mathit{m}({\sf T})={\sf T},~\mathit{m}({\sf F})={\sf F},~\mathit{m}(X\unlhd a\unrhd Y)=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(Y)),

and the auxiliary functions
L_{a},R_{a}:\mathcal{T}_{A}\to\mathcal{T}_{A}
are defined as follows, for
b\in A
:

\displaystyle L_{a}({\sf T})=R_{a}({\sf T})={\sf T},~~L_{a}({\sf F})=R_{a}({\sf F})={\sf F},

\displaystyle L_{a}(X\unlhd b\unrhd Y)=\begin{cases}L_{a}(X)&\text{if $b=a$},\\
L_{a}(X)\unlhd b\unrhd L_{a}(Y)&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle R_{a}(X\unlhd b\unrhd Y)=\begin{cases}R_{a}(Y)&\text{if $b=a$},\\
R_{a}(X)\unlhd b\unrhd R_{a}(Y)&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
A^{s}
be the set of strings over
A
with the property
that each
\sigma\in A^{s}
contains no multiple occurrences of the same atom.
Hence, for all
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
, each path in
\mathit{mfe}(P)
from the root
to a leaf has a sequence of labels of the form
\sigma\in A^{s}
.
It is clear that not all memorising evaluation trees can be expressed in
MFEL
, a simple example
is
({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf T}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf F})
.
Three examples of memorising evaluation trees in
\mathit{mfe}({\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}})
:

\displaystyle\mathit{mfe}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)=\mathit{m}(\mathit{fe}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a))=\mathit{m}(\mathit{fe}(a)[{\sf T}\mapsto\mathit{fe}(a),{\sf F}\mapsto\mathit{fe}(a)])

\displaystyle\hskip 48.93872pt=\mathit{m}(({\sf T}\unlhd a\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf T}\unlhd a\unrhd{\sf F}))=\mathit{m}(L_{a}({\sf T}))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}({\sf F}))=\mathit{fe}(a),

\displaystyle\mathit{mfe}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg a)={\sf T}\unlhd a\unrhd{\sf T}=\mathit{fe}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}),

\displaystyle\mathit{mfe}((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg b))=({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T}).

The last example suggests that all memorising evaluation trees
with complete traces
abB_{i}
with
i=1,..,4
and
B_{i}\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F}\}
can be expressed in
MFEL
and
below we show that each such evaluation tree can be expressed
as
\mathit{mfe}((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}t_{1})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}t_{2}))
with

t_{j}\in\{b,\neg b,b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F},b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}\}
. We note that this last evaluation tree cannot be expressed
in
FFEL
and return to expressiveness issues in Section
8
.

Definition 4.3
.

The binary relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}}

on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
is called
memorising full valuation congruence

and is defined by
P=_{\mathit{mfe}}Q\iff\mathit{mfe}(P)=\mathit{mfe}(Q)
.

The following lemma is an adaptation of
[
6
, La.3.5]
, a
detailed proof is given in Appendix
B
.

Lemma 4.4
.

The relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}}
is a congruence.

Definition 4.5
.

Memorising Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic
(
MFEL
)
is the fully evaluated
left-sequential logic that satisfies no more consequences than those of
\mathit{mfe}
-equality, i.e.,
for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}\models P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{mfe}}Q.

In Table
2
we provide a set
EqMFEL
of axioms for
MFEL
that is an extension
of
EqFFEL
with the axiom (
M1
). In the remainder of this section
we show that
EqMFEL
axiomatises
MFEL
.

Lemma 4.6

(Soundness)
.

For all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\vdash P=Q~\Longrightarrow~\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}\models P=Q
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.4
, the relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}}
is a congruence on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
, so it suffices to show
that all closed instances of the
EqMFEL
-axioms satisfy
=_{\mathit{mfe}}
.
By Lemma
2.7
(soundness of
FFEL
), we only have to prove this for axiom (
M1
),
thus for all
P,Q,R\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,

\mathit{m}(\mathit{fe}((P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R))=\mathit{m}(\mathit{fe}((\neg P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R))).

We prove this in detail in Appendix
B
.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

The equations in Table
3
are consequences of
EqMFEL
.

Proof.

First,

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{M1}^{\prime}}}{{=}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x
, where
\eqref{M1}^{\prime}
is the dual of
(
M1
).

(

):

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{C4}}}{{=}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf .

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{C4}}}{{=}}x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)
.

Each of (
C3
) and (
C4
) follows with
Prover9

with (the default) options
lpo
and
unfold
in 1s,
but we could not find any short, handwritten proofs.
∎

In order to prove completenss of
EqMFEL
, we use normal forms.

Definition 4.8
.

Let
\sigma\in A^{s}
and
a\in A
. Then
P
is a
\bm{\sigma}
-normal form
if

\sigma=\epsilon
and
P\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F}\}
, or if

\sigma=a\rho
and
P=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{1})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2})
with both

P_{1}
and
P_{2}

\rho
-normal forms.

For
\sigma=a\rho\in A^{s}
, each
\sigma
-normal form

(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{1})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2})
yields the perfect
a\rho
-tree
with root
a
, left child
P_{1}
and right child
P_{2}
.
Note that there are
2^{(2^{|\sigma|})}

\sigma
-normal forms.

We will often denote
\sigma
-normal forms by
P_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma},R_{\sigma}
, etc.
In many coming proofs we apply induction on
P_{\sigma}
, that is, we induct on the length of

\sigma
, often using the template
\sigma=a\rho
with
a\in A
for the induction step(s).
We call this approach “by induction on
\sigma
” for short.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
\sigma\in A^{s}
.
The
\sigma
-normal forms
\bm{{\sf T}_{\sigma}}
and
\bm{{\sf F}_{\sigma}}
are defined by

{\sf T}_{\epsilon}={\sf T}
,
{\sf T}_{a\rho}=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{\rho})
,
and

{\sf F}_{\epsilon}={\sf F}
,
{\sf F}_{a\rho}=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})
.

We will also use terms
\bm{\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\sigma}}
defined by
\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\epsilon}={\sf F}
and

\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{a\rho}=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

For all
\sigma\in A^{s}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\vdash\neg{\sf F}_{\sigma}={\sf T}_{\sigma},~\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\sigma}={\sf F}_{\sigma}
.
If
\sigma=a\rho
, then

\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\vdash\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\sigma}=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}.

Proof.

The first consequence follows by induction on
\sigma
. If
\sigma=\epsilon
, this is trivial,
and if
\sigma=a\rho
, then

\neg{\sf F}_{a\rho}=(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg{\sf F}_{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{C3},\text{IH}}}{{=}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{\rho})={\sf T}_{a\rho}.

The last consequence follows from
associativity and axiom (
FFEL7
):

\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}=\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}
,
hence

\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{a\rho}=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}))=((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{FFEL8}}}{{=}}((\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}))=(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho}).

Finally,
\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\sigma}={\sf F}_{\sigma}

follows by induction on
\sigma
.
If
\sigma=\epsilon
this is immediate, and if
\sigma=a\rho
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{a\rho}

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho})

by idempotence and the last consequence

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})

by IH

\displaystyle={\sf F}_{a\rho}.

∎

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\sigma\in A^{s}
, then

P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\sigma}=P_{\sigma}
and

P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{\sigma}=P_{\sigma}

are consequences of
EqMFEL
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma

The two cases for
\sigma=\epsilon
are immediate. If
\sigma=a\rho
,

\displaystyle P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{a\rho}

\displaystyle=((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\rho}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\rho})

by Lemma
4.10

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho}))

by associativity and Lemma
4.10

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho}))

by (
C4
)

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\rho})

by IH

\displaystyle=P_{a\rho}.

Next, it follows from (
C3
)
(in Table
3
) by induction on
\nu\in A^{s}
that
\neg P_{\nu}
is provably equal to a

\nu
-normal form. By Lemma
4.10
,

P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{a\rho}=\neg(\neg P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{a\rho})=P_{a\rho}.

∎

We define an auxiliary opertor
h(x,y,z)
that preserves the
property that in the evaluation of closed terms the left-right order is always respected
(modulo memorising occurrences of atoms).

Definition 4.12
.

The ternary operator
\bm{h(x,y,z)}
on terms over
\Sigma_{\textup{$\textrm{FEL}$}}(A)
is defined by

h(x,y,z)=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z).

Hence,
h(a,P_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma})
is a
a\sigma
-normal form (adopting the notational convention that

P_{\sigma}
and
Q_{\sigma}
are
\sigma
-normal forms).
In particular,
{\sf T}_{a\rho}=h(a,{\sf T}_{\rho},{\sf T}_{\rho})
and
{\sf F}_{a\rho}=h(a,{\sf F}_{\rho},{\sf F}_{\rho})
.

Lemma 4.13
.

The following equations are consequences of
EqMFEL
:

1.

h(x,y,z)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w=h(x,(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w,~z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w)
,

2.

h(x,y,z)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w=h(x,(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w,~z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w)
.

Proof.

Consequence 1 follows from consequence 2 and
\neg h(x,y,z)=h(x,\neg y,\neg z)

(which follows easily from (
C3
)).
Consequence 2 follows with
Prover9
with options
lpo
and
unfold
in 2s.
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\sigma\in A^{s}
, then

P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}_{\sigma}
and

P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}={\sf T}_{\sigma}

are consequences of
EqMFEL
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma
. The case
\sigma=\epsilon
is immediate, and if
\sigma=a\rho
then

\displaystyle P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}

\displaystyle=h(a,Q_{\rho},R_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}

\displaystyle=h(a,(Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F},~R_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by Lemma
4.13
.1

\displaystyle=h(a,(Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F},~{\sf F}_{\rho})

by IH

\displaystyle=h(a,Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F},~{\sf F}_{\rho})

by Lemma
4.11

\displaystyle=h(a,{\sf F}_{\rho},{\sf F}_{\rho})

by IH

\displaystyle={\sf F}_{a\rho}.

The case for
P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}
follows in a similar way
(with help of Lemma
4.13
.2).
∎

In order to compose
\sigma
-normal forms, we introduce some notation.

Definition 4.15

(
str(P)
and
\sigma\gg\rho
)
.

For
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
, the string
\bm{str(P)}\in A^{s}
is defined by
str({\sf T})=str({\sf F})=\epsilon
,

str(a)=a

(a\in A)
,
str(\neg P)=str(P)
, and

str(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)=str(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)=str(P)\gg str(Q)
, where

\_\gg\_:A^{s}\times A^{s}\to A^{s}

is the operation that filters out the atoms of the left argument in the right argument:

\sigma\gg\epsilon=\sigma
and
\sigma\gg a\rho=\begin{cases}\sigma\gg\rho&\text{if $a$ occurs in $\sigma$},\\
\sigma a\gg\rho&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Observe that
\sigma\gg a\rho=(\sigma\gg a)\gg\rho
and
\epsilon\gg\rho=\rho
.

Lemma 4.16
.

In
EqMFEL
,
\sigma
-normal forms in
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
are provably closed under
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
composition.

Proof.

We first consider
P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}
and prove this case by induction
on
\sigma
.

For
\sigma=\epsilon
,
{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}=R_{\nu}

is immediate, and
{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}={\sf F}_{\nu}

follows from (
FFEL7
) and Lemma
4.14
.

For
\sigma=a\rho
it suffices to prove that

h(a,P_{\rho},Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}=h(a,~P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu})

because by induction, both
P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}
and
Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}
have a
provably equal
(\rho\gg\nu)
-normal form:

\displaystyle h(a,P_{\rho},Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}

\displaystyle=h(a,(P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu})

by Lemma
4.13
.1

\displaystyle=h(a,(P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}).

by Lemma
4.14

\displaystyle=h(a,P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}).

by Lemma
4.11

The case for
P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}
follows in a similar way (with help of Lemma
4.13
.2).
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

For each
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
there is a unique
\sigma
-normal form

Q
such that
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\vdash P=Q
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
P
, restricting to NNFs. For
P\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F},a,\neg a\mid a\in A\}

this is trivial:
a=h(a,{\sf T},{\sf F})
and
\neg a=h(a,{\sf F},{\sf T})
.
For the cases
P=P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2}
and
P=P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2}
this follows from Lemma
4.16
.

Uniqueness follows from the facts that
str(P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2})
is fixed and that
syntactically different normal forms have different
evaluation trees: for
F(x,y,z)=h(y,x,z)
, their
F
-representation mimics the tree structure.
∎

Theorem 4.18

(Completeness)
.

The logic
MFEL
is axiomatised by
EqMFEL
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.6
,
EqMFEL
is sound. For completeness, assume
P_{1}=_{\mathit{mfe}}P_{2}
.
By Lemma
4.17
there are unique
\sigma_{i}
-normal forms
Q_{\sigma_{i}}
such that
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\vdash P_{i}=_{\mathit{mfe}}Q_{\sigma_{i}}
. By assumption and soundness,
Q_{\sigma_{1}}=Q_{\sigma_{2}}
.
Hence,
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\vdash P_{1}=Q_{\sigma_{1}}=P_{2}
.
∎

5
MFEL with undefinedness:
\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}

In this section we define
\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}
, the extension of
MFEL
with
{\sf U}
.
First, we formally define memorising
{\sf U}
-evaluation trees.
Given the detailed accounts in Sections
3
and
4
, this extension
is rather simple and straightforward. We define memorising
{\sf U}
-evaluation trees,
give equational axioms for the equality of these trees and prove
a completeness result.

Definition 5.1
.

The evaluation trees
{\sf T},{\sf F},{\sf U}\in{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}
are
memorising
\bm{{\sf U}}
-evaluation trees
.

The evaluation tree
(X\unlhd\underline{a}\unrhd Y)\in{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}
is a

memorising
\bm{{\sf U}}
-evaluation tree
over
A

if both
X
and
Y
are memorising
{\sf U}
-evaluation trees that do not contain the label
\underline{a}
.

We interpret propositional expressions in
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
as memorising
{\sf U}
-evaluation trees
by extending the function
\mathit{mfe}
(Definition
4.2
).

Definition 5.2
.

The unary
memorising full evaluation
function
\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}:{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}\to{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}

is defined by

\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}(P)=\mathit{m}^{\sf U}(\mathit{fe}^{\sf U}(P)),

where the auxiliary function
\mathit{m}^{\sf U}:{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}\to{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}
is defined as
follows, for
a\in A
:

\mathit{m}^{\sf U}(B)=B\text{ for }B\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F},{\sf U}\},~\mathit{m}^{\sf U}(X\unlhd\underline{a}\unrhd Y)=\mathit{m}^{\sf U}(L^{\sf U}_{a}(X))\unlhd\underline{a}\unrhd\mathit{m}^{\sf U}(R^{\sf U}_{a}(Y)),

and the auxiliary functions
L^{\sf U}_{a},R^{\sf U}_{a}:{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}\to{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}
are defined as follows, for
b\in A
:

\displaystyle L^{\sf U}_{a}(B)=R^{\sf U}_{a}(B)=B\text{ for }B\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F},{\sf U}\},

\displaystyle L^{\sf U}_{a}(X\unlhd\underline{b}\unrhd Y)=\begin{cases}L^{\sf U}_{a}(X)&\text{if $b=a$},\\
L^{\sf U}_{a}(X)\unlhd\underline{b}\unrhd L^{\sf U}_{a}(Y)&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle R^{\sf U}_{a}(X\unlhd\underline{b}\unrhd Y)=\begin{cases}R^{\sf U}_{a}(Y)&\text{if $b=a$},\\
R^{\sf U}_{a}(X)\unlhd\underline{b}\unrhd R^{\sf U}_{a}(Y)&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Definition 5.3
.

The binary relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}

on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
is called
memorising full
\bm{{\sf U}}
-valuation congruence

and is defined by
P=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}Q\iff\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}(P)=\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}(Q)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

The relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}
is a congruence.

Proof.

The proof of Lemma
4.4
(in Appendix
B
) requires one extra base case

X={\sf U}
for all sub-proofs, which follows trivially.
∎

Definition 5.5
.

Memorising Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic with undefinedness
(
MFEL
U
)

is the fully evaluated
left-sequential logic with undefinedness that satisfies no more consequences than those of
\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}
-equality, i.e.,
for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}\models P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}Q.

We extend
EqMFEL
to
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
with the two axioms

\neg{\sf U}={\sf U}
and
{\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}
, so all

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
-results of Section
3
hold in
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Lemma 5.6

(Soundness)
.

For all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P=Q~\Longrightarrow~\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}\models P=Q
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.4
, the relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}
is a congruence on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,
so it suffices to show that all closed instances of the
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
-axioms satisfy
=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}
,
which follows easily (cf. the proof of Lemma
4.6
and, for the
{\sf U}
-axioms,
the proof of Lemma
3.6
).
∎

In order to prove completeness of
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
, we use the
\sigma
-normal forms

{\sf U}_{\sigma}
from Definition
3.8
for
\sigma\in A^{s}
,
thus
{\sf U}_{\epsilon}={\sf U}
and
{\sf U}_{a\rho}=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho}
.
By induction on
\sigma
and Lemma
3.10
.2-3,

For all
\sigma\in A^{s}
,

\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash\neg{\sf U}_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
.

(
\ddagger
)

We further use the ternary operator
h(x,y,z)=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)

(Definition
4.12
) on terms over
\Sigma_{\textup{$\textrm{FEL}$}}^{\sf U}(A)
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For each
a\in A
and
\sigma=a\rho\in A^{s}
,

\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash{\sf U}_{a\rho}=h(a,{\sf U}_{\rho},{\sf U}_{\rho})
.

Proof.

Derive
a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho}=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{La.\ref{la:hulpje}.3}}}{{=}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\rho})=h(a,{\sf U}_{\rho},{\sf U}_{\rho})
.
∎

In the following, we extend previous results from Section
4
to
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
.

Lemma 5.8

(extension of La.

)
.

by IH

Lemma 5.9

(extension of La.
4.14
)
.

Let
\sigma\in A^{s}
, then

P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
and

P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}={\sf U}_{\sigma}

are consequences of
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Proof.

If
P_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
, this follows by Lemma
3.9
.
If
P_{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
, this follows by induction on
\sigma
.
We first consider the case
P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
.
If
\sigma=\epsilon

the statement is trivial. If
\sigma=a\rho
then

\displaystyle P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}

\displaystyle=h(a,Q_{\rho},R_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}

\displaystyle=h(a,(Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U},~R_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U})

by Lemma
4.13
.2

\displaystyle=h(a,(Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U},~{\sf U}_{\rho})

by Lemma
4.14
and IH

\displaystyle=h(a,Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U},~{\sf U}_{\rho})

by Lemma
4.11

\displaystyle=h(a,{\sf U}_{\rho},{\sf U}_{\rho})

by IH

\displaystyle={\sf U}_{a\rho}.

by Lemma
5.7

The case for
P_{a\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}
follows in a similar way
(with help of Lemma
