.1).
∎

Lemma 5.10

(extension of La.
4.16
)
.

In
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
,
\sigma
-normal forms in
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
are provably closed under

{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
composition, and each of these
compositions determines a unique
\nu
-normal form.

Proof.

We first consider
P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}
and distinguish three cases.

Case

P_{\sigma}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
. By Lemma
3.9
,

{\sf U}_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}={\sf U}_{\sigma}
.

Case

P_{\sigma},R_{\nu}\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
. By Lemma
4.16
, there is a
(\sigma\gg\nu)
-normal form
s.t.
P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}=Q_{\sigma\gg\nu}
.

Case

P_{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
and
R_{\nu}={\sf U}_{\nu}
. We prove this case by induction
on
\sigma
:

If
\sigma=\epsilon
,
{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}={\sf U}_{\nu}
is immediate, and

{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}={\sf U}_{\nu}
follows from (
FFEL7
) and Lemma
3.9
.

If
\sigma=a\rho\in A^{s}
, it suffices to prove that

h(a,P_{\rho},Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}=h(a,~P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu})

because by induction, both
P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}
and
Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}
have a
provably equal
(\rho\gg\nu)
-normal form:

\displaystyle h(a,P_{\rho},Q_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}

\displaystyle=h(a,(P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu})

by Lemma
4.13
.1

\displaystyle=h(a,(P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}_{\rho})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}).

by Lemma
4.14

\displaystyle=h(a,P_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu},~Q_{\rho}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf U}_{\nu}).

by Lemma
4.11

The proof for
P_{\sigma}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R_{\nu}
follows in a similar way (with help of Lemma
4.13
.2).
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

For each
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
there is a unique
\sigma
-normal form

Q
s.t.
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P=Q
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
P
, restricting to NNFs. For
P\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F},{\sf U},a,\neg a\mid a\in A\}

this is trivial:
a=h(a,{\sf T},{\sf F})
and
\neg a=h(a,{\sf F},{\sf T})
.
For the cases
P=P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2}
and
P=P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2}
this follows from Lemma
5.10
.

Uniqueness follows from the facts that for
\sigma
-normal form
P_{1}
and
\rho
-normal form
P_{2}
,
the normal forms of
P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2}
and
P_{1}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P_{2}
are unique by Lemma
5.10
,
and that
syntactically different normal forms have different
evaluation trees: for
F(x,y,z)=h(y,x,z)
, their
F
-representation mimics the tree structure.
∎

Theorem 5.12

(Completeness)
.

The logic
\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}
is axiomatised by
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.6
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
is sound. For completeness, assume
P_{1}=_{\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}}P_{2}
.
By Lemma
5.11
there are unique
\sigma_{i}
-normal forms
Q_{\sigma_{i}}

such that
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P_{i}=_{\mathit{mfe}}Q_{\sigma_{i}}
. By assumption and soundness,

Q_{\sigma_{1}}=Q_{\sigma_{2}}
. Hence,
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P_{1}=Q_{\sigma_{1}}=P_{2}
.
∎

6
Two-Valued Conditional
FEL
(
C
\ell
FEL
2
) and
C
\ell
FEL

Two-valued Conditional
FEL
(
C
\ell
FEL
2
) and three-valued Conditional
FEL
(
C
\ell
FEL
) are both sublogics of Conditional logic (defined in 1990 by Guzmán and Squier),
as was shown in
[
3
]
.
2
2

2

In fact it was shown in
[
3
]
that
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)
, and hence the axiom

(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)=(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)
, which is a
characteristic axiom of Conditional logic, implies commutativity
of
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
. We return to this point in Section
8
.

C
\ell
FEL
2
is equivalent to a sequential version of propositional logic that refutes the absorption laws.
The characteristic property of
C
\ell
FEL
2
and
C
\ell
FEL
is that sequential propositions are identified
according to the commutativity of
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
(and
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
).
For both the case without
{\sf U}
and with
{\sf U}
, we define a semantics based on ordered memorising
trees, give equational axioms for their equality, and prove a completeness result.
We note that
C
\ell
FEL
seems most close to ‘‘three-valued left-sequential propositional logic”
as one can get, the cost being the loss
of absorption.
3
3

3

Note that
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}
is a consequence of absorption:

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)={\sf F}
.
This equation plays an important role in Static FEL (see Section
7
)
.

For
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
, its
alphabet
{\alpha(P)}\subset A
is defined by

\alpha({\sf T})=\alpha({\sf F})=\alpha({\sf U})=\emptyset
,

\alpha(a)=\{a\}
,
\alpha(\neg P)=\alpha(P)
and
\alpha(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)=\alpha(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)=\alpha(P)\cup\alpha(Q)
.

We further assume that the atoms in
A
are ordered
a_{1}<a_{2}<...<a_{n}<a_{n+1}...
, notation
(A,<)
.

Definition 6.1
.

A^{s}_{o}
is the set of strings in
A^{*}
whose elements are ordered according to
(A,<)
.

So,
A^{s}_{o}\subset A^{s}
.
We reserve the symbols

\beta,\gamma
for strings
b_{1}b_{2}...b_{k}b_{k+1}
in
A^{s}_{o}
that satisfy
b_{i}<b_{i+1}

(not necessarily neighbours in
(A,<)
),
and for such a string
\beta
we write
\{\beta\}
for its
set of atoms.

Below we define evaluation functions for
C
\ell
FEL
2
and
C
\ell
FEL
that use disjunctions

\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P
in order to identify
P
with a term that respects

A^{s}_{o}
, where the terms
\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}
are defined in Definition
4.9
.

Definition 6.2
.

The
conditional evaluation
function

\mathit{clfe}:{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}\to\mathcal{T}_{A}
is defined by

\mathit{clfe}(P)=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P),\text{ where $\beta\in A^{s}_{o}$ satisfies
$\{\beta\}=\alpha(P)$}.

The
conditional evaluation
function

\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}:{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}\to{\mathcal{T}_{A}^{\sf U}}
is defined by

\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}(P)=\begin{cases}\mathit{mfe}^{\sf U}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P)&\text{if $P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}$ and $\beta\in A^{s}_{o}$ satisfies
$\{\beta\}=\alpha(P)$,}\\
{\sf U}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

These evaluation functions are well-defined because
\beta
is uniquely determined
by
\alpha(P)
and
(A,<)
.
For each
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
, the evaluation tree
\mathit{clfe}(P)

can be called a “
\beta
-tree”, its complete traces agree with
\beta
, and
its leaves determine the evaluation result of
P
.

Example 6.3
.

Four typical examples with
\beta=ab
, thus

\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})
:

\displaystyle\mathit{clfe}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)

\displaystyle=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a))=\mathit{mfe}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b),

\displaystyle\mathit{clfe}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)

\displaystyle=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a))=\mathit{mfe}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b),

\displaystyle\mathit{clfe}((b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)

\displaystyle=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b))=\mathit{mfe}((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b),

\displaystyle\mathit{clfe}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b))

\displaystyle=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)))=\mathit{mfe}((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b).

Definition 6.4
.

The relation
=_{\mathit{clfe}}\subset{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}\times{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,
conditional full valuation congruence
, is
defined by
P=_{\mathit{clfe}}Q
if
\mathit{clfe}(P)=\mathit{clfe}(Q)
. The relation

=_{\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}}\subset{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}\times{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,
conditional full
\bm{{\sf U}}
-valuation congruence
,
is defined by
P=_{\mathit{clfe}}Q
if
\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}(P)=\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}(Q)
.

Lemma 6.5
.

The relations
=_{\mathit{clfe}}
and
=_{\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}}
are both congruences.

Proof.

Because
=_{\mathit{mfe}}
is a congruence (Lemma
4.4
), so is
=_{\mathit{clfe}}
.
This follows from the fact that
=_{\mathit{mfe}}

preserves the alphabet of its arguments, that is, if
P=_{\mathit{mfe}}Q
then
\alpha(P)=\alpha(Q)
.
E.g., assume for
P,Q,R\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
that
P=_{\mathit{clfe}}Q
, then

\mathit{clfe}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R)=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{La.\ref{la:Mcongruence}}}}{{=}}\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R))=\mathit{clfe}(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R)
, where the last equality holds because
\beta
satisfies

\{\beta\}=\alpha(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R)=\alpha(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R)
.

In a similar way it follows by Lemma
5.4
that
=_{\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}}
is a congruence.
∎

Definition 6.6
.

Conditional Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic
(
C
\ell
FEL
2
)
is the fully evaluated
left-sequential logic that satisfies no more consequences than those of
\mathit{clfe}
-equality, i.e.,
for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{C$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\models P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{clfe}}Q.

Conditional Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic with undefinedness
(
C
\ell
FEL
)

is the fully evaluated
left-sequential logic with undefinedness that satisfies no more consequences than those of
\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}
-equality, i.e.,
for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{C$\ell$FEL}$}\models P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{clfe}^{\sf U}}Q.

We define the following sets
of axioms for
C
\ell
FEL
2
and
C
\ell
FEL
:

EqC
\ell
FEL
2

\displaystyle=\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}\cup\{x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x\},

\displaystyle\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}

\displaystyle=\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}\cup\{x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x\}.

Lemma 6.7

(Soundness)
.

1.
For all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\vdash P=Q~\Longrightarrow\textup{$\textrm{C$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\models P=Q
,
and

2.
For all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}^{\!{\sf U}}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}\vdash P=Q~\Longrightarrow\textup{$\textrm{C$\ell$FEL}$}\models P=Q
.

Proof.

1.
By Lemma
6.5
, the relation
=_{\mathit{clfe}}
is a congruence on
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
, so it suffices to show
that all closed instances of the
EqC
\ell
FEL
2
-axioms satisfy
=_{\mathit{clfe}}
.
By Lemma
4.6
, we only have to prove this for the axiom
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x
.
Let
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
and let
\beta\in A^{s}_{o}
be such that
\{\beta\}=\alpha(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)
.
In Appendix
C
we prove
that
\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q))=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P))
,
hence
\mathit{clfe}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)=\mathit{clfe}(Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P)
.

2.

Soundness of
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
follows in a similar way (see also Appendix
C
).
∎

We first prove completeness of
EqC
\ell
FEL
2
, and then discuss the completeness of
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\vdash h(x,~h(y,z,u),~h(y,v,w))=h(y,~h(x,z,v),~h(x,u,w))
.

Proof.

By commutativity and associativity of
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
, and (
C4
) (distributivity):

\displaystyle h(x,~

\displaystyle h(y,z,u),~h(y,v,w))

\displaystyle=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}u)))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}v)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w)))

\displaystyle=((x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}u)))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}v))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w)))

\displaystyle=((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}u)))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}v))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w)))

\displaystyle=(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}v)))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}u)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}w)))

\displaystyle=h(y,~h(x,z,v),~h(x,u,w)).

∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\sigma\in A^{s}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
be a permutation of
\sigma
.
Then for each
\sigma
-normal
P_{\sigma}
there is a
\sigma^{\prime}
-normal form
Q
such that

\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\vdash P_{\sigma}=Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma
.
If
\sigma=\epsilon
or
\sigma=a\in A
, this is trivial.

Assume
\sigma=a_{0}a_{1}...a_{k}
and
\sigma^{\prime}=b_{0}b_{1}...b_{k}
. If
a_{0}=b_{0}
,
we are done by induction.
Otherwise,
a_{0}=b_{\ell}
with
\ell\neq 0
and by induction,
P_{\sigma}=h(a_{0},R_{\rho^{\prime}},S_{\rho^{\prime}})
with
\rho^{\prime}=b_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime}
for some
\rho^{\prime\prime}\in A^{s}
.
By Lemma
6.8
,
h(a_{0},R_{\rho^{\prime}},S_{\rho^{\prime}})=h(b_{0},V_{a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime}},W_{a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime}})
.
Now, either we are done, or
there is a permuation
p()
of
a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime}
such that
b_{0}p(a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime})=\sigma^{\prime}
.
By induction,
h(b_{0},V_{a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime}},W_{a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime}})=h(b_{0},V_{p(a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime})}^{\prime},W_{p(a_{0}\rho^{\prime\prime})}^{\prime})=Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.
∎

Definition 6.10
.

A
\bm{\beta}
-normal form
is a
\sigma
-normal form (Definition
4.8
)
with
\sigma\in A^{s}_{o}
. We denote
\beta
-normal forms by
P_{\beta}
,
Q_{\beta}
, etc.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
\beta\in A^{s}_{o}
and
\sigma
a permutation of
\beta
.
Then in
C
\ell
FEL
2
, each
\sigma
-normal form
P_{\sigma}
is provably equal to a
\beta
-normal form.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.9
.
∎

Theorem 6.12

(Completeness)
.

The logic
C
\ell
FEL
2
is axiomatised by
EqC
\ell
FEL
2
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.7
,
EqC
\ell
FEL
2
is sound. For completeness, assume
P_{1}=_{\mathit{clfe}}P_{2}
, thus,
for some
\beta\in A^{s}_{o}
,
\alpha(P_{i})=\{\beta\}
.
By assumption and Lemma
6.11
there is a
\beta
-normal form
Q_{\beta}
such that

\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\vdash P_{1}=Q_{\beta}=P_{2}
.
∎

The completeness of
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
can be proved in the same way.
In comparison with
C
\ell
FEL
2
, there is only one additional
\beta
-normal form
{\sf U}
.

Theorem 6.13

(Completeness)
.

The logic
C
\ell
FEL
is axiomatised by
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

It follows that
C
\ell
FEL
is equivalent to Bochvar’s well-known
three-valued logic
[
8
]
.
In Comment
7.5
(on independent axiomatisations)
we discuss this equivalence, or more precisely,
the equivalence of
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
and the equational axiomatisation of Bochvar’s logic
discussed in
[
1
]
.

7
Static
FEL
(
SFEL
), short-circuit logic, and independence

In this section we define Static
FEL
(
SFEL
), the strongest two-valued
FEL
we consider and
which is equivalent to a sequential of propositional logic.

SFEL
is axiomatised by adding
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}
to the axioms of (two-valued)
C
\ell
FEL
2
.
It is immediately clear that
SFEL
cannot be extended with
{\sf U}
:
{\sf F}={\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf U}
.
In
SFEL
, the difference between
full
and
short-circuit
evaluation
has disappeared and therefore we can reuse results on
static short-circuit logic
, which
we briefly introduce.
Finally, we review all
FEL
-axiomatisations and give
simple equivalent alternatives with help of
Prover9
, all of which are independent.

We assume
(A,<)
and
A^{s}_{o}
(Definition
6.1
)
and consider terms whose alphabet is constrained by some
\beta\in A^{s}_{o}
and
for which we define an evaluation funtion
\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}
.

Definition 7.1
.

For any
\beta\in A^{s}_{o}
,
{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}=\{P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}\mid\alpha(P)\subset\{\beta\}\}
.
For any
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}
,

\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}(P)=\mathit{mfe}(\widetilde{}{\sf F}_{\beta}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P).

The relation
=_{\mathit{sfe},\beta}\subset{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}\times{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}
is called
static full valuation

\bm{\beta}
-congruence
and is defined by
P=_{\mathit{sfe},\beta}Q
if
\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}(P)=\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}(Q)
.

The crucial difference between
\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}
and the evaluation function
\mathit{clfe}

(Definition
6.2
) concerns their domain: for
\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}(P)
it is only
required that
\alpha(P)
is a subset of
\{\beta\}
.

Example 7.2
.

If
\beta=ab
, we find that

\displaystyle({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T})=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}({\sf T})=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(\neg b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}),

\displaystyle({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\;=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(a)=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}((b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}a)=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(h(b,a,a)),

\displaystyle({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\;=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(b)=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(b~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})))=\mathit{sfe}_{ab}(h(a,b,b)).

Definition 7.3
.

Static Fully Evaluated Left-Sequential Logic
SFEL
is the fully evaluated
left-sequential logic that satisfies for any
\beta\in A^{s}_{o}

no more consequences than those of
\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}
-equality, i.e.,
for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{SFEL}$}\models P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}}Q.

We define the following set
EqSFEL
of axioms for
SFEL
:

\textup{$\textrm{EqSFEL}$}=\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL${}_{2}$}$}\cup\{x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}\}.

In order to prove completeness of
EqSFEL
, we reuse a result from
Short-Circuit Logic
(SCL),
a family of logics based on the connectives
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
and
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
that prescribe
short-circuit evaluation:
once the evaluation result of a propositional expression is determined, evaluation stops. Typically,

a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}b
has as its semantics the evaluation tree in which atom
b
is only evaluated if
a
evaluates to
true
:
a
b
{\sf T}
{\sf F}
{\sf F}

The
SCL
-family comprises the counterparts of the
FEL
s
presented in this paper, that is,
FSCL
,
MSCL
,
C
\ell
SCL
2
and
SSCL
, and in all these logics
the connective
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
is definable, as was noted in
[
19
,
5
,
3
]
:

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y.

We return to these facts in Section
8
.

\displaystyle{\sf F}

\displaystyle=\neg{\sf T}

(Mem1)

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y

\displaystyle=\neg(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y)

(Mem2)

\displaystyle{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x

\displaystyle=x

(Mem3)

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)

\displaystyle=x

(Mem4)

\displaystyle(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}z

\displaystyle=(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}z))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y

\displaystyle=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x

(Comm)

EqSSCL
, a set of axioms for
SSCL
(axioms (

)–(
Mem5
) are used in Appendix
B
)

Theorem 7.4

(Completeness)
.

The logic
SFEL
is axiomatised by
EqSFEL
.

Proof.

For
\sigma\in A^{s}
, let
{\cal S}_{\sigma}
be the set of closed SCL-terms with atoms
in
\sigma
.
In
[
5
]
, it is argued on p.21 that the static evaluation trees of all
P\in{\cal S}_{\sigma}

are perfect binary trees, where each level characterises the evaluation
of a single atom in
\sigma
, and it easily follows that
\mathit{sse}_{\sigma}(P)=\mathit{sfe}_{\sigma}(P^{\prime})
if
P^{\prime}

is obtained from
P
by
replacing the short-circuit connectives by their fully evaluated counterparts.

According to
[
5
]
,
EqSSCL
is the set of axioms in Table
4
that axiomatises

SSCL
.
Three consequences of
EqSSCL
are
{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf F}
,
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x
, and

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y
. We recall from
[
5
]
a derivation of the last one:

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}({\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y
.

In
[
5
, Thm.6.11]
it is proven that for all
P,Q\in{\cal S}_{\sigma}
,

\textup{$\textrm{EqSSCL}$}\vdash P=Q\iff\mathit{sse}_{\sigma}(P)=\mathit{sse}_{\sigma}(Q)
.
Identifying
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}
, it suffices to show that
EqSSCL
and
EqSFEL
are equivalent,
and this follows quickly with
Prover9
. Hence, for all
P,Q\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}
,

\textup{$\textrm{EqSFEL}$}\vdash P=Q\iff P=_{\mathit{sfe}_{\beta}}Q
.
∎

Finally, we provide simple equivalent alternatives for all
FEL
-axiomatizations
with help
Prover9
, where we use the superscript
{\sf U}
in their names to indicate the extension
with the two axioms
\neg{\sf U}={\sf U}
and
{\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf U}
.
Moreover, each of these alternative axiomatisations is independent, which
quickly follows with help of
Mace4
.

EqFFEL

and
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

Define
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}_{2}=\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\setminus\{\eqref{FFEL1}\}
, where (
FFEL1
) is the axiom
{\sf F}=\neg{\sf T}
.
With
Prover9
it quickly follows that

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}_{2}\vdash{\sf F}=\neg{\sf T}
, and according to

Mace4
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}^{\sf U}_{2}

is independent. Hence,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}_{2}
is also independent.

and
\textup{$\textrm{EqMFEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

.

With

it follows that

.

EqC
\ell
FEL
2

and
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
.

\text{CF}=\{x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x\}\cup\text{MF}\setminus\{\eqref{MF6}\}

EqSFEL
.

Define

\text{SF}=\{x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}\}\cup\text{MF}\setminus\{\eqref{MF6}\}
.
According to
Prover9
,
\text{SF}\vdash\textup{$\textrm{EqSFEL}$}
, and according to
Mace4
,

SF
is independent.

Thus, in addition to its independence, the alternative axiomatisation
MF
is elegant,
due to its simplicity and conciseness, and this is also the case for the stronger axiomatisations.
We end this section with a comment on
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
and
\text{CF}^{\sf U}
.

.
