(S4)

\displaystyle(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z

\displaystyle=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)

(S6)

\displaystyle{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x

\displaystyle=x

(S7)

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)

\displaystyle=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y

(S8)

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y

\displaystyle=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x

(S9)

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)

\displaystyle=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)

(S10)

\displaystyle{\sf U}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x

\displaystyle={\sf U}

(S11)

The ‘missing’ axiom (S5) defines the connective

~\mathbin{\begin{picture}\put(-0.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\put(-0.1,0.57){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}~}
(full left-sequential implication,
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}\put(-0.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\put(-0.1,0.57){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}~}y=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y
) and is not relevant here.
According to
Prover9
, this axiomatisation is equivalent with
\textup{$\textrm{EqC$\ell$FEL}$}^{\sf U}
.
However, without axiom (S9), this set is not an axiomatisation of
\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}
, and therefore
we prefer
\text{CF}^{\sf U}
and
CF
.
Finally, we note that according to
Mace4
,
axiom (S6) is derivable from the remaining axioms, which
are independent. Moreover, the same is true for axiom (S10).

8
Discussion and conclusions

As mentioned earlier, this paper is a continuation of
[
19
]
,
in which
FFEL
was introduced
and which contained the first completeness proof for the short-circuit logic
FSCL
(Free SCL).
We introduced six more
fully evaluated sequential logics
(
FEL
s), two of which
are not new:
C
\ell
FEL
, which is equivalent to the logic of Bochvar
[
8
]
, and
SFEL
, which
is equivalent to
SSCL
and to propositional logic, see
[
4
]
.

We could have defined the fully evaluated connectives
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}
, and thus the FELs, with help
of the ternary connective
x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}z
, the so-called “conditional” that expresses
“
if
y
then
x
else
z
” and with which
Hoare characterised the propositional calculus in
1985
[
13
]
(also using both constants
{\sf T}
and
{\sf F}
).
4
4

4

However, in 1948, Church introduced
conditioned disjunction

[p,q,r]
in
[
10
]

as a primitive connective for the propositional calculus, which expresses the same
as Hoare’s conditional
p\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}q\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}r
, and proved the same result.

With this connective, short-circuit conjunction
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}
and disjunction
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}
are simply defined
by

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptx~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y={\sf T}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}y,

and negation by

\neg x={\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf T}
.
The conditional connective naturally characterises short-circuit evaluation and has a notation
that supports equational reasoning, which led us to define
short-circuit logics
(
SCL
s)
based on this connective
[
4
,
3
]
.
SCLs can be characterised by their efficiency,
in the sense that atoms are not evaluated if their
evaluation is not needed to determine the evaluation of a term as a whole.
From that perspective FELs might seem rather inefficient, but this is not necessarily so. We first
elaborate on SCLs and then return to this point.

Short-circuit evaluation and SCLs.

The short-circuit logic
FSCL
is defined by the equational theory of terms
over the signature
\Sigma_{\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}}=\{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg,{\sf T},{\sf F}\}
that is generated by the two
equations
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F}
and
\neg x={\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf T}
,
and the set of four basic axioms that we call
CP
:

x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}{\sf T}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}y=x,\hskip 10.00002ptx\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}{\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}y=y,\hskip 10.00002pt{\sf T}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F}=x,\hskip 10.00002ptx\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}(y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}u)\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}v=(x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}v)\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}(x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}u\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}v).

Write
\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)
for the set of these six axioms.
5
5

5

Short-circuit disjunction
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y
is defined by

\neg(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y)
.

As an example, the associativity of
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
is
quickly derived from
\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)
, and thus holds in
FSCL
:

\displaystyle(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}z

\displaystyle=z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}(y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F})\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F}

by definition

\displaystyle=(z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F})\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}(z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}{\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F})

by
x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}(y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}u)\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}v=(x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}v)\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}(x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}u\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}v)

\displaystyle=(z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F})\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F}

by
x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}{\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}y=y

\displaystyle=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}z).

by definition

Each of the
FEL
s discussed in this paper is related to one of the
SCL
s
defined in
[
4
,
3
]
.

Memorising
SCL (
MSCL
)
is defined as the restriction to
\Sigma_{\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}}

of the equational theory of
\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)
extended with the “memorising” axiom

x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}(z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}u\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}(v\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}w))=x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}(z\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}u\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}w)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{($y$ is ``memorised").}

(43)

As an example,
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x=x
is easily derivable from these seven axioms,
and so are the axioms (
Mem1
)–(
Mem5
) in Table
4
.
More generally, the validity of any equation in a particular
SCL
can be checked in the associated
CP
-system: either a proof can be
found (often with help of
Prover9
), or
Mace4
finds a counter model.
We finally note that in
[
6
]
,
SCL
s were extended with a constant
{\sf U}
for the
third truth value
undefined
and the defining axiom
x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}{\sf U}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}y={\sf U}
.
A complete axiomatisation for
\textup{$\textrm{MSCL}$}^{\sf U}
was given, but an axiomatisation of
\textup{$\textrm{FSCL}$}^{\sf U}
is not yet known,
not even when restricted to closed terms, see
[
6
, Conject.8.1]
.

FEL
s and
SCL
s.

As was noted in
[
19
]
,
the connective
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
can be defined
using short-circuit connectives and the constant
{\sf F}
(due to the
FSCL
-law
{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x={\sf F}
):

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y.

(44)

It is easy to prove that

\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)+\eqref{eq:rhs}\vdash x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}({\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F})

and the latter equation is perhaps more intuitive than (
44
).
The addition of
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}({\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf F})
and
\neg x={\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf T}
to
CP
,
say
\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)
,
implies that
\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)\vdash\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}
.
Similarly, the addition of axiom (
43
) to
\textup{$\textrm{CP}$}(~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}},\neg)

implies that
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x=x
and (
M1
) in Table
2

are derivable.
Like in the case of
SCL
s, the validity of any equation
in a particular
FEL
can be easily checked in the associated
CP
-system.
The addition of
{\sf U}
is in the case of
FEL
s simple.
For
\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}
there are normal forms
{\sf U}_{\sigma}
with
\sigma\in A^{*}
, and for
\textup{$\textrm{MFEL}$}^{\sf U}
,
normal forms
{\sf U}_{\sigma}
with
\sigma\in A^{s}
. In the case of
C
\ell
FEL
, these all reduce to
{\sf U}
.

Side effects.

A quote from
[
19
]
: We find that some programming languages offer full
left-sequential connectives, which motivated the initial investigation of FEL.
We claim that FEL has a greater value than merely to act as means of writing certain SCL-terms
using fewer symbols.
The usefulness of a full evaluation strategy lies in the
increased
predictability of the state of the environment after a (sub)term has been
evaluated. In particular, we know that the side effects of all the atoms in the term have occurred.
To determine the state of the environment after the evaluation of a FEL-term in a given environment,
we need only compute how each atom in the term transforms the environment.
It is not necessary to compute the evaluation of any of the atoms.
With SCL-terms in general we must know to what the first atom evaluates in order to determine which atom
is next to transform the environment. Thus to compute the state of the environment after the
evaluation of an SCL-term we must compute the evaluation result of each atom that transforms the environment
and we must compute the transformation of the environment for each atom that affects it.

Expressiveness.

We call a perfect evaluation tree
uniform
if each complete trace
contains the same sequence of atoms.
By definition, each
FEL
defines a certain equality on the
domain of uniform evaluation trees.
In
FFEL
and
\textup{$\textrm{FFEL}$}^{\sf U}
, some uniform evaluation trees can be expressed, but for
example not

({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T}),

which is expressible in
MFEL
by
h(a,b,\neg b)=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg b)
,
and it easily follows that
each
uniform tree can be expressed in
MFEL
.
Of course, in
SFEL
, which is equivalent to
SSCL
,
with
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}={\sf F}
, each uniform
evaluation tree can be compared to a truth table for propositional logic and
it is possible to define a semantics for
SFEL
based on an equivalence
\equiv

on evaluation trees that identifies each (sub)tree with only
{\sf F}
-leaves with
{\sf F}
, and each (sub)tree
with only
{\sf T}
-leaves with
{\sf T}
.
Then, any
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A,\beta}}
has a representing evaluation tree without such subtrees, for example,

\displaystyle\mathit{sfe}_{abc}((a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}c))

\displaystyle=(({\sf T}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf T})\unlhd b\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf F}))\unlhd a\unrhd(({\sf T}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd b\unrhd({\sf T}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf F}))

\displaystyle=(({\sf T}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf T})\unlhd b\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf F}))\unlhd a\unrhd(({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T})\unlhd c\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F}))

(by Lemma
6.8
)

\displaystyle=({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf T}\unlhd c\unrhd{\sf F}).

(by
\textup{$\textrm{SFEL}$}/{\equiv}
)

In
MSCL
, the latter tree is the evaluation tree of
(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}c)
,
see
[
6
]
for a detailed explanation.

As a final point, we note that there are left-sequential, binary connectives for which the distinction
between short-circuit
evaluation and full evaluation does not exist, such as the
left-sequential biconditional
and the

left-sequential exclusive or
, defined by Cornets de Groot in
[
11
]
by

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-0.98,0.58){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}({\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf T})

and
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\oplus$}\put(-1.02,0.56){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=({\sf F}\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}y\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}{\sf T})\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\lhd\;$}}x\scalebox{0.78}{\raisebox{0.1pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\;\rhd\;$}}y
, respectively.
It immediately follows that
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\oplus$}\put(-1.02,0.56){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-0.98,0.58){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y
and
\neg(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-0.98,0.58){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-0.98,0.58){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y
.
In
[
17
]
, the notations
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-0.98,0.58){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\oplus$}\put(-1.02,0.56){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}
are used, and below we use
the symbols
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-1.38,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}}
and
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\oplus$}\put(-1.62,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}
, expressing only left-sequentiality.
Regarding expressiveness, we note that in
FFEL
extended with
~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-1.38,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}
,
the evaluation tree of
a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-1.38,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}b
is
({\sf T}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd({\sf F}\unlhd b\unrhd{\sf T})
.
However, it easily follows that not all uniform
evaluation trees can be expressed in
FFEL
with
{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-1.38,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}}
.
6
6

6

Suppose
A=\{a\}
. Then there are
5\cdot 4^{4}\cdot 3^{3}
terms composed of four elements of

\{a,\neg a,a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T},a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}\}
and the three connectives in
\{~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}},~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}},~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-1.38,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}\}

that have evaluation trees of depth 4. It is not hard to prove that these terms represent all
closed terms with evaluation trees of depth 4.
However, there are
2^{16}
such trees.

Of course, in
MFEL
and all stronger
FEL
s, the left-sequential biconditional
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.5,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\leftrightarrow$}\put(-1.38,0.02){{\small$\leftarrow$}}\end{picture}}y

is definable by
h(x,y,\neg y)
.

Related work.

In the recent paper
[
3
]
,
Conditional logic
(Guzmán and
Squier
[
12
]
) is viewed as a short-circuit logic, and its two-valued variant

C
\ell
SCL
2
is located in between MSCL and
SSCL.
7
7

7

The name
C
\ell
SCL
was chosen because CSCL is already used for “Contractive
SCL” in
[
4
]
, the short-circuit logic that (only) contracts adjacent atoms:
a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}a=a
, but

(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}b)
and
a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}b
have different evaluation trees.

In
[
3
]
it was also established that Conditional logic defines a fully evaluated
sublogic that is equivalent to the strict three-valued logic of Bochvar
[
8
]
;
this is the logic we now call
C
\ell
FEL
.
It is remarkable that also for the two-valued case,
the typical axiom
x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y=y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}x
is a consequence of the
distinguishing C
\ell
SCL-axiom

(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)=(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}x)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.54,1.54){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,1.54){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\wedge$}\put(-0.54,-0.2){{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\circle*{0.6}}}\put(-0.54,-0.2){\circle{0.6}}\end{picture}}y).

This particular consequence led us to
C
\ell
FEL
2
,
a
FEL
in between
MFEL
and
SFEL
that can
be motivated as a sublogic of C
\ell
SCL.
Thus, we locate
C
\ell
FEL
in a hierarchy of two-valued FELs, and we derive its existence
and motivation both from the three-valued logic
C
\ell
SCL
,
and thus from Bochvar’s logic,
and from the consideration to adopt a full evaluation strategy.

With regard to the family of
FEL
s, it remains a challenge to find related work.
This is also the case for the (somewhat larger) family of SCLs, apart from
[
12
]
and
our own papers on SCLs.

Appendix A
Proofs - Section
2

Correctness of
\bm{f}
.

To prove that
f:{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}\to\textup{FNF}
is indeed a normalization function we need to
prove that for all
FEL
-terms
P
,
f(P)
terminates,
f(P)\in\textup{FNF}
and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f(P)=P
. To arrive at this result, we prove several
intermediate results about the functions
f^{n}
and
f^{c}
, roughly in the
order in which their definitions were presented in Section
2
. For
the sake of brevity we will not explicitly prove that these functions
terminate. To see that each function terminates consider that a termination
proof would closely mimic the proof structure of the lemmas dealing with the
grammatical categories of the images of these functions.

Lemma A.1
.

For any
P^{\sf F}
and
P^{\sf T}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash P^{\sf F}=P^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}
and
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash P^{\sf T}=P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}
.

Proof.

We prove both claims simultaneously by induction. In the base case we have

{\sf F}={\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}
by (
FFEL5
), which is equal to
{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}
by (
FFEL8
) and (
FFEL1
). The base case for the
second claim follows from that for the first claim by duality.

For the induction we have
a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf F}=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})
by the induction hypothesis and the result follows from
(
FFEL4
). For the second claim we again appeal to duality.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

The following equations can be derived by equational logic and
EqFFEL
.

1.

x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

2.

\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})=\neg(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))

Proof.

\displaystyle x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))

\displaystyle=x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}((\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}z)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by (
FFEL4
) and Lemma
2.6
.
1

\displaystyle=(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by (
FFEL4
) and Lemma
2.6
.
1

\displaystyle=\neg(\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by Lemma
2.6
.
1

\displaystyle=(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}y)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(z~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}),

by (
FFEL2
)

\displaystyle\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})

\displaystyle=\neg x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by (
FFEL10
)

\displaystyle=\neg(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))

by (
FFEL2
) and (
FFEL3
)

\displaystyle=\neg(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg(\neg y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg{\sf T}))

by (
FFEL8
) and (
FFEL1
)

\displaystyle=\neg(x~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(y~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})).

by (
FFEL2
)

∎

Lemma A.3
.

For all
P\in\textup{FNF}
, if
P
is a

{\sf T}
-term then
f^{n}(P)
is an
{\sf F}
-term, if it is an
{\sf F}
-term
then
f^{n}(P)
is a
{\sf T}
-term, if it is a
{\sf T}
-
*
-term then so is

f^{n}(P)
, and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}(P)=\neg P.

Proof.

We start with proving the claims for
{\sf T}
-terms, by induction on
P^{\sf T}
.
In the base case
f^{n}({\sf T})={\sf F}
. It is immediate that
f^{n}({\sf T})

is an
{\sf F}
-term. The claim that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}({\sf T})=\neg{\sf T}

is immediate by (
FFEL1
). For the inductive case we have that
f^{n}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}(P^{\sf T})
, where we may assume that

f^{n}(P^{\sf T})
is an
{\sf F}
-term and that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}(P^{\sf T})=\neg P^{\sf T}
. The grammatical claim now follows immediately from the induction
hypothesis. Furthermore, noting that by the induction hypothesis we may assume
that
f^{n}(P^{\sf T})
is an
{\sf F}
-term, we have:

\displaystyle f^{n}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}(P^{\sf T})

by definition

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(f^{n}(P^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(f^{n}(P^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by Lemma
2.6
.
1

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}(P^{\sf T})

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg P^{\sf T}

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=\neg(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T}).

by (
FFEL3
) and (
FFEL2
)

For
{\sf F}
-terms we prove our claims by induction on
P^{\sf F}
. In the base
case
f^{n}({\sf F})={\sf T}
. It is immediate that
f^{n}({\sf F})
is a

{\sf T}
-term. The claim that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}({\sf F})=\neg{\sf F}
is
immediate by the dual of (
FFEL1
). For the inductive case we have that

f^{n}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf F})=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}(P^{\sf F})
, where we may assume
that
f^{n}(P^{\sf F})
is a
{\sf T}
-term and
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}(P^{\sf F})=\neg P^{\sf F}
. It follows immediately from the induction hypothesis that

f^{n}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf F})
is a
{\sf T}
-term. Furthermore, noting that by
the induction hypothesis we may assume that
f^{n}(P^{\sf F})
is a

{\sf T}
-term, we prove the remaining claim as follows:

\displaystyle f^{n}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf F})

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}(P^{\sf F})

by definition

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(f^{n}(P^{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(f^{n}(P^{\sf F})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})

by the dual of Lemma
2.6
.
1

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}(P^{\sf F})

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg P^{\sf F}

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=\neg(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf F}).

by (
FFEL3
) and (
FFEL2
)

To prove the lemma for
{\sf T}
-
*
-terms we first verify that the auxiliary
function
f^{n}_{1}
returns a
*
-term and that for any
*
-term
P
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}_{1}(P)=\neg P
. We show this by induction on the number of

\ell
-terms in
P
. For the base cases, i.e., for
\ell
-terms, it is
immediate that
f^{n}_{1}(P)
is a
*
-term. If
P
is an
\ell
-term with a
positive determinative atom we have:

\displaystyle f^{n}_{1}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T}

by definition

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))

by Lemma
A.2
.
2

\displaystyle=\neg(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T}).

by Lemma
A.1

If
P
is an
\ell
-term with a negative determinative atom the proof proceeds
the same, substituting
\neg a
for
a
and applying (
FFEL3
) where
needed. For the inductive step we assume that the result holds for
*
-terms
with fewer
\ell
-terms than
P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{d}
and
P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c}
. We
note that each application of
f^{n}_{1}
changes the main connective (not
occurring inside an
\ell
-term) and hence the result is a
*
-term. Derivable
equality is, given the induction hypothesis, an instance of (the dual of)
(
FFEL2
).

With this result we can now see that
f^{n}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*})
is indeed a

{\sf T}
-
*
-term. Furthermore we find that:

\displaystyle f^{n}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*})

\displaystyle=P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{n}_{1}(Q^{*})

by definition

\displaystyle=P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg Q^{*}

as shown above

\displaystyle=(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg Q^{*}

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg Q^{*}

by Lemma
2.6
.
2

\displaystyle=\neg P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}\neg Q^{*}

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=\neg(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*}).

by (
FFEL2
) and (
FFEL3
)

Hence for all
P\in\textup{FNF}
,
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{n}(P)=\neg P
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

For any
{\sf T}
-term
P
and
Q\in\textup{FNF}
,
f^{c}(P,Q)
has the same
grammatical category as
Q
and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P,Q)=P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of the first argument. In the base case we see
that
f^{c}({\sf T},P)=P
and hence has the same grammatical category as
P
.
Derivable equality follows from (
FFEL5
).

For the induction step we make a case distinction on the grammatical category
of the second argument. If the second argument is a
{\sf T}
-term we have that

f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})
, where
we assume that
f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})
is a
{\sf T}
-term and
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})=P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}
. The grammatical
claim follows immediately from the induction hypothesis. The claim about
derivable equality is proved as follows:

\displaystyle f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})

by definition

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T})

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})

by Lemma
2.6
.
3

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}.

by Lemma
A.1

If the second argument is an
{\sf F}
-term we assume that
f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf F})
is an
{\sf F}
-term and that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf F})=P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf F}
. The grammatical claim follows
immediately from the induction hypothesis. Derivable equality is proved as
follows:

\displaystyle f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf F})

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{c}(P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf F})

by definition

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf F})

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by Lemma
A.2
.
1

\displaystyle=(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf F}.

by Lemma
A.1

Finally, if the second argument is a
{\sf T}
-
*
-term then
f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{*})=f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{*}
. The fact that this is a
{\sf T}
-
*
-term follows from the fact that

f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf T},Q^{\sf T})
is a
{\sf T}
-term as was shown above.
Derivable equality follows from the case where the second argument is a

{\sf T}
-term and (
FFEL4
).
∎

Lemma A.5
.

For any
{\sf T}
-
*
-term
P
and
{\sf F}
-term
Q
,
f^{c}(P,Q)
is an

{\sf F}
-term and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P,Q)=P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q.

Proof.

By (
FFEL4
) and Lemma
A.4
it suffices to show that

f^{c}_{2}(P^{*},Q^{\sf F})
is an
{\sf F}
-term and that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}_{2}(P^{*},Q^{\sf F})=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf F}
. We prove this by induction on
the number of
\ell
-terms in
P^{*}
. In the base cases, i.e.,
\ell
-terms, the
grammatical claims follow from Lemma
A.4
. The claim about derivable
equality in the case of
\ell
-terms with positive determinative atoms follows
from Lemma
A.4
and (
FFEL4
). For
\ell
-terms with negative
determinative atoms it follows from Lemma
A.4
, Lemma
A.1
,
(
FFEL7
), (
FFEL4
) and (
FFEL8
).

For the induction step we assume the claims hold for any
*
-terms with fewer

\ell
-terms than
P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{d}
and
P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c}
. In the case of
conjunctions we have
f^{c}_{2}(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{d},R^{\sf F})=f^{c}_{2}(P^{*},f^{c}_{2}(Q^{d},R^{\sf F}))
and the grammatical claim follows from the induction
hypothesis (applied twice). Derivable equality follows from the induction
hypothesis and (
FFEL4
).

For disjunctions we have
f^{c}_{2}(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c},R^{\sf F})=f^{c}_{2}(P^{*},f^{c}_{2}(Q^{c},R^{\sf F}))
and the grammatical claim follows from the induction
hypothesis (applied twice). The claim about derivable equality is proved as
follows:

\displaystyle f^{c}_{2}(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c},R^{\sf F})

\displaystyle=f^{c}_{2}(P^{*},f^{c}_{2}(Q^{c},R^{\sf F}))

by definition

\displaystyle=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{c}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{\sf F})

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{c}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F}))

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf F})

by Lemma
A.2
.
1

\displaystyle=(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{\sf F}.

by Lemma
A.1

∎

Lemma A.6
.

For any
{\sf F}
-term
P
and
Q\in\textup{FNF}
,
f^{c}(P,Q)
is an

{\sf F}
-term and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P,Q)=P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q.

Proof.

We make a case distinction on the grammatical category of the second argument.
If the second argument is a
{\sf T}
-term we proceed by induction on the first
argument. In the base case we have
f^{c}({\sf F},P^{\sf T})=f^{n}(P^{\sf T})

and the result is by Lemma
A.3
, Lemma
A.1
, (
FFEL7
)
and (
FFEL8
). In the inductive case we have
f^{c}(a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}P^{\sf F},Q^{\sf T})=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{c}(P^{\sf F},Q^{\sf T})
, where we assume that

f^{c}(P^{\sf F},Q^{\sf T})
is an
{\sf F}
-term and
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P^{\sf F},Q^{\sf T})=P^{\sf F}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}
. The result now follows
from the induction hypothesis and (
FFEL4
).

If the second argument is an
{\sf F}
-term the proof is almost the same, except
that we need not invoke Lemma
A.3
or (
FFEL8
) in the base case.

Finally, if the second argument is a
{\sf T}
-
*
-term we again proceed by
induction on the first argument. In the base case we have
f^{c}({\sf F},P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*})=f^{c}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*},{\sf F})
. The grammatical
claim now follows from Lemma
A.5
and derivable equality follows
from Lemma
A.5
and (
FFEL7
). For for the inductive case the
results follow from the induction hypothesis and (
FFEL4
).
∎

Lemma A.7
.

For any
{\sf T}
-
*
-term
P
and
{\sf T}
-term
Q
,
f^{c}(P,Q)
has the same
grammatical category as
P
and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P,Q)=P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q.

Proof.

By (
FFEL4
) it suffices to prove the claims for
f^{c}_{1}
, i.e., that

f^{c}_{1}(P^{*},Q^{\sf T})
has the same grammatical category as
P^{*}
and that

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}_{1}(P^{*},Q^{\sf T})=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}
. We prove this by
induction on the number of
\ell
-terms in
P^{*}
. In the base case we deal with

\ell
-terms and the results follow from Lemma
A.4
and
(
FFEL4
).

For the inductive cases we assume that the results hold for any
*
-term with
fewer
\ell
-terms than
P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{d}
and
P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c}
. In the case
of conjunctions the results follow from the induction hypothesis and
(
FFEL4
). In the case of disjunctions the grammatical claim follows from
the induction hypothesis. For derivable equality we have:

\displaystyle f^{c}_{1}(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c},R^{\sf T})

\displaystyle=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}f^{c}_{1}(Q^{c},R^{\sf T})

by definition

\displaystyle=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{c}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{\sf T})

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{c}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T}))

by Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle=(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}{\sf T})

by Lemma
2.6
.
3

\displaystyle=(P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{c})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{\sf T}.

by Lemma
A.1

∎

Lemma A.8
.

For any
P,Q\in\textup{FNF}
,
f^{c}(P,Q)
is in
FNF
and

\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P,Q)=P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q.

Proof.

By the four preceding lemmas it suffices to show that
f^{c}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*},R^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}S^{*})
is in
FNF
and that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*},R^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}S^{*})=(P^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{*})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(R^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}S^{*})
. By (
FFEL4
), in turn, it suffices to prove that

f^{c}_{3}(P^{*},Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{*})
is a
*
-term and that
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f^{c}_{3}(P^{*},Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{*})=P^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}(Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{*})
. We
prove this by induction on the number of
\ell
-terms in
R^{*}
. In the base
case we have that
f^{c}_{3}(P^{*},Q^{\sf T}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{\ell})=f^{c}_{1}(P^{*},Q^{\sf T})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R^{\ell}
. The results follow from Lemma
A.7
and
(
FFEL4
).

For the inductive cases we assume that the results hold for all
*
-terms with
fewer
\ell
-terms than
R^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}S^{d}
and
R^{*}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}S^{c}
. For
conjunctions the result follows from the induction hypothesis and
(
FFEL4
) and for disjunctions it follows from Lemma
A.7
and
(
FFEL4
).
∎

Theorem
2.9
.

For any
P\in{\mathcal{SP}_{\!A}}
,
f(P)
terminates,
f(P)\in\textup{FNF}
and
\textup{$\textrm{EqFFEL}$}\vdash f(P)=P
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
P
. If
P
is an atom, the result is by
(
FFEL5
) and (
FFEL6
). If
P
is
{\sf T}
or
{\sf F}
the result is
by identity. For the induction we assume that the result holds for all

FEL
-terms of lesser complexity than
P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q
and
P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q
. The
result now follows from the induction hypothesis, Lemma
A.3
, Lemma

A.8
and (
FFEL2
).
∎

Correctness of
\bm{g}
.

Theorem
2.18
.

For all
P\in\textup{FNF}
,
g(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
, i.e.
g(\mathit{fe}(P))
is syntactically equal
to
P
for
P\in\textup{FNF}
.

Proof.

We first prove that for all
{\sf T}
-terms
P
,
g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
,
by induction on
P
. In the base case
P={\sf T}
and we have

g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(P))=g^{\sf T}({\sf T})={\sf T}=P
. For the
inductive case we have
P=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}
and

\displaystyle g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T})\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T}))

by definition of
\mathit{fe}

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T}))

by definition of
g^{\sf T}

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=P.

Similarly, we see that for all
{\sf F}
-terms
P
,
g^{\sf F}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
, by induction on
P
. In the base case
P={\sf F}
and we have

g^{\sf F}(\mathit{fe}(P))=g^{\sf F}({\sf F})={\sf F}=P
. For
the inductive case we have
P=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf F}
and

\displaystyle g^{\sf F}(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{\sf F}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf F})\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf F}))

by definition of
\mathit{fe}

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{\sf F}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf F}))

by definition of
g^{\sf F}

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf F}

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=P.

Now we check that for all
\ell
-terms
P
,
g^{\ell}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
.
We observe that either
P=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}
or
P=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}
. In the first case we have

\displaystyle g^{\ell}(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{\ell}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T})\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T})[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F}])

by definition of
\mathit{fe}

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T}))

by definition of
g^{\ell}

\displaystyle=a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}

as shown above

\displaystyle=P.

In the second case we have that

\displaystyle g^{\ell}(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{\ell}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T})[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F}]\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T}))

by definition of
\mathit{fe}

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(Q^{\sf T}))

by definition of
g_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\neg a~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q^{\sf T}

as shown above

\displaystyle=P.

We now prove that for all
*
-terms
P
,
g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
, by
induction on
P
modulo the complexity of
\ell
-terms. In the base case we are
dealing with
\ell
-terms. Because an
\ell
-term has neither a cd nor a dd we
have
g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(P))=g^{\ell}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
, where the first
equality is by definition of
g^{*}
and the second was shown above. For the
induction we have either
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
or
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
. In
the first case note that by Theorem
2.15
,
\mathit{fe}(P)
has a cd and no
dd. So we have

\displaystyle g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{*}(\textup{cd}_{1}(\mathit{fe}(P))[\triangle_{1}\mapsto{\sf T},\triangle_{2}\mapsto{\sf F}])~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{*}(\textup{cd}_{2}(\mathit{fe}(P)))

by definition of
g^{*}

\displaystyle=g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(Q))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(R))

by Theorem
2.15

\displaystyle=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=P.

In the second case, again by Theorem
2.15
,
\mathit{fe}(P)
has a dd and no
cd. So we have that

\displaystyle g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{*}(\textup{dd}_{1}(\mathit{fe}(P))[\triangle_{1}\mapsto{\sf T},\triangle_{2}\mapsto{\sf F}])~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{*}(\textup{dd}_{2}(\mathit{fe}(P)))

by definition of
g^{*}

\displaystyle=g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(Q))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(R))

by Theorem
2.15

\displaystyle=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R

by induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=P.

Finally, we prove the theorem’s statement by making a case distinction on the
grammatical category of
P
. If
P
is a
{\sf T}
-term, then
\mathit{fe}(P)
has only

{\sf T}
-leaves and hence
g(\mathit{fe}(P))=g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
,
where the first equality is by definition of
g
and the second was shown
above. If
P
is an
{\sf F}
-term, then
\mathit{fe}(P)
has only
{\sf F}
-leaves and
hence
g(\mathit{fe}(P))=g^{\sf F}(\mathit{fe}(P))=P
, where the first
equality is by definition of
g
and the second was shown above. If
P
is a

{\sf T}
-
*
-term, then it has both
{\sf T}
and
{\sf F}
-leaves and hence,
letting
P=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R
,

\displaystyle g(\mathit{fe}(P))

\displaystyle=g^{\sf T}(\textup{tsd}_{1}(\mathit{fe}(P))[\triangle\mapsto{\sf T}])~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{*}(\textup{tsd}_{2}(\mathit{fe}(P)))

by definition of
g

\displaystyle=g^{\sf T}(\mathit{fe}(Q))~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}g^{*}(\mathit{fe}(R))

by Theorem
2.17

\displaystyle=Q~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}R

as shown above

\displaystyle=P,

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs - Section
4

In order to show that the relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}}
is indeed a congruence, we first
repeat a part of
[
6
, La.3.3]
,
except that we generalise its clause 5 to clause 2 below.
8
8

8

The proof of clause 5 of
[
6
, La.3.3]
(p.266) contains two errors in Case
c=a
,
correct is:
“and by induction
L_{a}(X_{1}[{\sf T}\mapsto Y])=L_{a}(X_{1})[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y)]=L_{a}(X)[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y)]
” (cf. Case
b=a
in the proof
below and take
Z={\sf F}
).

Lemma B.1
.

For all
a\in A
,
f\in\{L,R\}
and
X,Y,Z\in\mathcal{T}_{A}
,

1.

\mathit{m}(f_{a}(X[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F},{\sf F}\mapsto{\sf T}]))=\mathit{m}(f_{a}(X))[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F},{\sf F}\mapsto{\sf T}]
,

2.

f_{a}(X[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])=f_{a}(X)[{\sf T}\mapsto f_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto f_{a}(Z)]
.

Proof.

Clause 1 is Lemma 3.3.4 in
[
6
]
.
We prove clause 2 by induction on the structure of
X
.
The base cases are trivial.

If
X=X_{1}\unlhd b\unrhd X_{2}
, distinguish the cases
b=a
and
b\neq a
:

Case
b=a
, subcase
f=L
:

L_{a}(X[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])=L_{a}(X_{1}[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])
and by
induction,
L_{a}(X_{1}[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])=L_{a}(X_{1})[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto L_{a}(Z)]
.
By
L_{a}(X)=L_{a}(X_{1})
, we are done.
The other subcase
f=R
follows in a similar way.

Case
b\neq a
, subcase
f=L
:

\displaystyle L_{a}(X[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])

\displaystyle=L_{a}(X_{1}[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])\unlhd b\unrhd L_{a}(X_{2}[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Z])

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH}}}{{=}}L_{a}(X_{1})[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto L_{a}(Z)]\unlhd b\unrhd L_{a}(X_{2})[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto L_{a}(Z)]

\displaystyle=L_{a}(X)[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto L_{a}(Z)].

The other subcase
f=R
follows in a similar way.
∎

Lemma
4.4
.

The relation
=_{\mathit{mfe}}
is a congruence.

Proof.

Define
\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X:\mathcal{T}_{A}\to\mathcal{T}_{A}
and

X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y,~X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){{$\widetilde{\vee}$}}\put(-0.46,1.3){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y:\mathcal{T}_{A}\times\mathcal{T}_{A}\to\mathcal{T}_{A}
by

\displaystyle\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X

\displaystyle=X[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F},{\sf F}\mapsto{\sf T}],

\displaystyle~X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y

\displaystyle=X[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Y[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F}]],

\displaystyle X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){{$\widetilde{\vee}$}}\put(-0.46,1.3){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y

\displaystyle=X[{\sf T}\mapsto Y[{\sf F}\mapsto{\sf T}],{\sf F}\mapsto Y].

Hence,
\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}(\mathit{fe}(P))=\mathit{fe}(\neg P)
,
\mathit{fe}(P)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}(Q)=\mathit{fe}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)
, and

\mathit{fe}(P)~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){{$\widetilde{\vee}$}}\put(-0.46,1.3){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}\mathit{fe}(Q)=\mathit{fe}(P~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.0){$\vee$}\put(-0.42,1.4){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Q)
.
It suffices to show that for
X,Y\in\mathcal{T}_{A}
, if
\mathit{m}(X)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime})
and
\mathit{m}(Y)=\mathit{m}(Y^{\prime})
, then

\mathit{m}(\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X)=\mathit{m}(\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X^{\prime})
,

\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y^{\prime})
, and

\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){{$\widetilde{\vee}$}}\put(-0.46,1.3){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){{$\widetilde{\vee}$}}\put(-0.46,1.3){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y^{\prime})
.

The case for
\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X
follows by case distinction on the form of
X
.
The base cases
X\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F}\}
are trivial, and if
X=X_{1}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}
, then

\mathit{m}(X)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime})
implies that

X^{\prime}=X_{1}^{\prime}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}^{\prime}
for some
X_{1}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{A}
, and

\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}))=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}^{\prime}))\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}))=\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}^{\prime})).

(Aux3)

Write
\bm{[\textit{{\small neg}}]}
for the leaf replacement
[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F},{\sf F}\mapsto{\sf T}]

and derive

\displaystyle\mathit{m}(\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X)

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}((X_{1}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2})\bm{[\textit{{\small neg}}]})

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}\bm{[\textit{{\small neg}}]}))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}\bm{[\textit{{\small neg}}]}))

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}^{\prime}\bm{[\textit{{\small neg}}]}))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}^{\prime}\bm{[\textit{{\small neg}}]}))

by La.
B.1
.1 and (
Aux3
)

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(\text{$\widetilde{\neg}$}X^{\prime}).

The case for
\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.3,0.0)\put(-0.6,0.2){$\wedge$}\put(-0.46,-0.0){\circle*{0.6}}\end{picture}}Y^{\prime})
:
for readability we split the proof obligation into two parts.

(A)
\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)
.
This follows by induction on the depth of
X
.
The base cases
X\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F}\}
are simple: note that
if
X={\sf T}
and
\mathit{m}(X)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime})
, then
X^{\prime}={\sf T}
and we are done, and similarly if
X={\sf F}
.

If
X=X_{1}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}
and
\mathit{m}(X)=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime})
, then by (
Aux3
),

X^{\prime}=X_{1}^{\prime}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}^{\prime}
for some
X_{1}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{A}
with

\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}))=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}^{\prime}))

and
\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}))=\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}^{\prime}))
.
Write

\displaystyle\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}

\displaystyle\text{ for $[{\sf T}\mapsto Y,{\sf F}\mapsto Y[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F}]]$},

\displaystyle\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $L_{a}(Y)$}]}

\displaystyle\text{ for $[{\sf T}\mapsto L_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto L_{a}(Y)[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F}]]$},

\displaystyle\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $R_{a}(Y)$}]}

\displaystyle\text{ for $[{\sf T}\mapsto R_{a}(Y),{\sf F}\mapsto R_{a}(Y)[{\sf T}\mapsto{\sf F}]]$}.

Derive

\displaystyle\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}((X_{1}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(X_{1}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]})

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}))

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1})\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $L_{a}(Y)$}]})\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2})\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $R_{a}(Y)$}]})

by La.
B.1
.2

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}L_{a}(Y))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}R_{a}(Y))

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}^{\prime})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}L_{a}(Y))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}^{\prime})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}R_{a}(Y))

by IH and (
Aux3
)

\displaystyle=...=\mathit{m}(X^{\prime}~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y).

(B)
\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)=\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y^{\prime})
.
This follows by induction on the depth of
X
.
The base cases
X\in\{{\sf T},{\sf F}\}
are trivial.
If
X=X_{1}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}
derive

\displaystyle\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}((X_{1}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y)

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(X_{1}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}\unlhd a\unrhd X_{2}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]})

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2}\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $Y$}]}))

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1})\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $L_{a}(Y)$}]})\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2})\bm{[\textit{{\small fulland} $R_{a}(Y)$}]})

by La.

.2

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}L_{a}(Y))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}R_{a}(Y))

\displaystyle=\mathit{m}(L_{a}(X_{1})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}L_{a}(Y^{\prime}))\unlhd a\unrhd\mathit{m}(R_{a}(X_{2})~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}R_{a}(Y^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=...=\mathit{m}(X~\mathbin{\begin{picture}(-0.4,0.0)\put(-0.8,0.0){$\widetilde{\wedge}$}\put(-0.66,-0.1){\circle*{0.7}}\end{picture}}Y^{\prime}).

The proof for the case
